ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u1	Dear fellow Members and fellow Members, ladies and gentlemen! I begin with the 21st session of the National Assembly. I am informed that today's meeting cannot be attended by the following Member and Members: Mr. Bevk [[from 15 hours to 15 hours]] Mr Branko Ficko [[from 11 a.m. on]] , Dr. Vinko Gorenak, Dr. Ljubica Jelusic, Mrs. Janja Klasinc, Mrs. Maša Kociper, Mr. Srečko Meh [[By 13:00 p.m.]] , Mr Mitja Meršol, Mrs Jasmina Opec, Mrs Alenka Pavlic, Mrs Marija Plavčak, Mr Marijan Pojbič, Mr Jakob Preseknik, Mr Ivan Simčič, Mr Bojan Starman, Mrs Irena Tavčar and Mrs Kristina Valenčič. I welcome everyone here. We're moving on to 13. At the end of the day, that's a first-hand proposal for a law on financial management. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly, and in relation to this proposal for a law, a group of 26 Members and Members with first-signed Jozet Tank requested that the National Assembly hold a general discussion. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government of Magi. Matthew Vraničar, Secretary of State of the Ministry of Finance. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u2	President, thank you for your word. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The bill before you governs the principles of action, organisation, tasks and powers of the newly-initiated financial administration. Collection of data and maintenance of the tax register and records, rights and specificities of the staff of the financial administration and other matters relating to the operation of the financial administration. Currently, in the Republic of Slovenia they collect compulsory duties from the Customs Administration of the Republic of Slovenia and from the Tax Administration of the Republic of Slovenia. With the entry of the Republic of Croatia into the European Union, customs control at the national border between Slovenia and Croatia has been lifted and the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has assessed that it is appropriate to continue the project already initiated in 2012 and to examine the appropriateness of the allocation of duties of the customs and tax services or to determine whether existing organisation is best in terms of the cost of functioning of the administrative authorities and ensuring effective collection of compulsory duties and the provision of appropriate services to the debtors. In view of the above, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has assessed that, in order to increase the efficiency of the collection of compulsory duties and to increase the rationality of the organisation of both services, it is appropriate for the tax and customs administrations to be merged into a single financial administration. The integration of both services also pursues the objectives of rationalising the implementation of public service procedures, optimising costs to date by separate bodies and making better use of the staff potential of both services. The unification of control mechanisms, databases will allow comprehensive and qualitative treatment of the debtors, as well as the reduction of administrative barriers to compliance with their obligations and, on the other hand, the reduction of the administrative burden on the financial administration. The merger will provide the new body with a uniform, efficient and rapid performance of tasks, equal treatment of all taxpayers, reduced costs on the part of taxpayers and the tax authority, equal access to tax services for all debtors and a more equal burden on all employees. The basic mission of the financial administration or its primary task is to collect compulsory duties, including the exercise of customs control. However, other tasks should be carried out by the future financial service if they are closely linked to this core task and it is rational to join the task of collecting compulsory duties. The aim of the bill is to establish a legal framework for the further development of a modernly organised administrative body that will ensure the implementation of the Taxation Act by following the principle of voluntary compliance with the obligations of the debtors, while ensuring that the taxable persons who do not fulfil their obligations are properly complied with within the time limits and in the manner laid down by the law. The expected effects of the merger relating to the performance of tasks are: reduction of administrative barriers for the debtors; unification of inspections; the possibility of accessing or establishing common databases to enable comprehensive and qualitative treatment of the debtors; better organisation of the joint body and more efficient organisation of work. The expected effects relating to the organisational aspect are the merger of internal organisational units performing the same tasks and the more efficient use of employees. Your Honor! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia proposes to the National Assembly to support the proposal for a law and to take a decision at first reading that the proposal for a law is suitable for further consideration. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u3	Thank you, too, for the introduction to the law. We are moving on to a presentation on the views of the groups of Members. Mr Franz Bogovic will present the position of the Group of the Slovenian People's Party.
ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u4	Good afternoon, distinguished colleagues and colleagues, distinguished president, distinguished secretary of state with co-workers! We are talking about the law on financial administration, and today is the first case under this law. Despite the fact that the Financial Administration Act has over 100 articles, it is more or less focused on one essential point of content: combining tax and customs administrations. Everything else is more or less the transposition of legislation and regulations under the current customs and tax laws. As most of us already know, the DURS merger talks, the tax administration says, and the customs administrations are already under way in the previous term and we have already had a bill prepared for this topic. Indeed, a long time ago, it was known that the entry of Croatia into the European Union would take place in mid-year and that this would also be followed by the need to reorganise the customs services in particular. Undoubtedly, it makes sense to merge the two bodies which have quite similar tasks, in this case, to collect compulsory duties, and to combine them under the same roof, so that the collection of all duties is also monitored and recorded under one roof. Thus, from this point of view, the integration or accession of the customs administration is not special if this means that we will be able to make effective use of the personnel that are now emerging as redundant, given that there is no longer any limit to Croatia's entry into the EU and that we will achieve the specific financial effects and savings that we still need in these times. It is right here that this practice from this coalition has already been recorded on several occasions at the outset. The section of the law, which reads "Assessing the financial implications of the draft state budget bill and other public funds", states that the merger will bring about approximately one million euros in costs, and only that savings are expected in connection with the merger. Savings — What Savings? We don't know if it's 1,000 euros, half a million, 10 million. Whether this means a smaller number of employees or an equal number of employees can do more, have better results, exercise better control. In the context of such a merger, one would think that the objectives of cost rationalisation as well as improvement of results should be very clearly defined and achieved. Only some reorganisations on paper do not bring progress, even less efficiency, but most often more costs and problems. In this case, pooling should be an opportunity to seek synergies for new, common systematisation, in order to avoid duplication of certain tasks. If this were the case, they would probably be talking about 10 to 20% lower costs or, of course, 15, 20% higher efficiency. In Slovenia alone, however, it is possible for the Ministry to prepare a merger of organs and all that can be seen from this conclusion is the forecast of one million additional costs. Just because some tangible goals in this merger are not detected, we feel in the Slovenian People's Party that the real purpose of this time's law is what we have already seen in some other rapid moves in the field of centralisation of the country. The abolition of tax exposures is planned because, as can be seen from some information, it is a matter of joining seven local tax administrations: Ptuj, Dravograd, Brežice, Kočevje, Velenje, Hrastnik and Postojna, as well as customs administrations to larger cities. And since the financial effects we have already learned are not even mentioned in the law, all of this reminds me of all the bad experiences we have from previous centralised decisions. Let us recall the police administrations that were abolished by the Pahor government. In the end, it turned out that savings are not practical and travel costs are increasing as employees drive here and where there are no essentials for improving security, better investigation of crimes, the result is that the residents of the place where the case was terminated have been left without representation from that body and also with many empty premises owned by the State. When we look at the problem a little bit wider than we have already seen in cutting and centralising, it turns out that it is not about saving at all, but about generating red tape in larger cities. We shut down offices, service services, important parts of the public service, we shut down the post office, shut down bank offices, out of the field of inspection services, we briefly return at least 50 years ago when people had to handle all relevant and official matters in larger urban centres. And most bizarrely, we don't even know whether we're actually saving or whether we're going to be workers who have worked in one place until then and who are also socially connected to it, we just have to drive elsewhere to their jobs, and we're going to cause extra transportation costs, and once this job is completed by every individual, there's going to be new jobs that are allowed in this way only in larger urban centres, which we don't agree with in the Slovenian People's Party. So if we are already moving people here and there, then there must be a very clear proven effect both in optimizing the process of work, either the financial impact that such changes had to entail, or the better and better quality of the service offered by this service. If this is not ensured, then I can easily claim that the whole law is behind only the very clear aspirations of the ruling policy after the centralisation of the country, which is now reflected in a number of other proposals. Let us just remember the Rooquitary proposals for the mass abolition of rural communities, remember the unfair property tax, the formation of administrative thefts, and so forth. The coalition should ask itself whether it really wants two Slovenias, urban and rural, rich and poor. And this law is also one of those who can either reduce or expose the gap even more. In the Slovenian People's Party, we expect that in this law, if its purpose is to really only unite tax and customs administrations, it is also very clear what the situation is today. And if the Ministry would later wish to implement organisational changes in terms of the abolition of offices, the exposition should come to the National Assembly for this mandate. The fact is that in the National Assembly we have been elected representatives of the people and only the National Assembly have been able to decide on such fundamental organisational changes in the area of tax collection. And if you want to stop a service once again for people who do not happen to live in one of the major cities in the country, then you will also have to take political responsibility for it. In the Slovenian People's Party, we will closely monitor the adoption of this law. Basic ideas are said to unite under the same roof of the customs and tax administration, we certainly follow and support this objective. However, we must be careful that such a law does not create new injustices across the country and that in some ways it leaves empty areas in the cities owned by the State, and in larger urban centres, someone does leasing contracts and in a similar way expensively sells the facilities to the State. This has been a practice in the past, if necessary, we will also serve on specific cases. However, I think it is right that we are once aware of this responsibility and even of what country we want to have, I mean that Slovenia is beautiful as long as it is decentralised. As long as many people can do their work, including in administrative bodies, near their home and thus influence the quality of life in this place for the wider environment. In the Slovenian People's Party, we believe that wisdom will prevail, that there are also MPs in the coalition who come from areas where they know very well what it means to step aside these services in the countryside, and that we will eventually adopt such a law that will follow the objectives which this law is intended for.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u7	Thank you, too. Mrs Iva Dimic will present the position of the New Slovenia Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u8	Honourable President of the National Assembly, representatives of the Ministry, colleague and fellow Members! The proposal for the Financial Administration Act was drafted and submitted to the parliamentary procedure in March last year by the previous government, in which Nova Slovenije participated as a coalition party. The current government has withdrawn the bill from the parliamentary procedure. With minor corrections, this bill was tabled in Parliamentary Procedure two months ago and thus lost at least half a year. By this time, the law could have had positive effects. The New Slovenia Christian Democrats support this bill's proposal, just as we supported it in the opposition, as we did in the previous government as a coalition party. The sharing of powers between tax and customs authorities in terms of rationalisation is irrelevant, in particular in the current and changed circumstances. As Croatia enters the European Union, there is no longer a need for customs control at the border with Croatia. Thus, staff resources which are already qualified to collect compulsory duties and other tasks under the responsibility of the customs and tax administration have remained available. On the other hand, the tax administration has been recording human resources malnutrition for some time now. The integration of the two authorities means the rationalisation of the work of the public administration. With appropriate staff allocations, it will be possible to ensure an effective system of collection of taxes, excise duties and other obligations. An important novelty brought by law is the establishment of the powers of financial management officials to carry out financial investigations and other tasks. The proposal for a law shall include inspectors, customs officers, investigators, controllers and expropriators. Such persons may use the powers set out in this Act in the performance of their financial management tasks, such as: seizure of documents, documents, data-collections and other matters, entry into and inspection of land, premises and facilities, inspection of goods and other items, management of means of transport, inspection and investigation of transport, and transfer of funds, security inspection, inspection of persons and other measures. Therefore, the powers will be very broad and reach or even exceed the police powers. Thus, financial administrations authorised will have in their hands an appropriate tool to detect tax evasion. With the entry of the Republic of Slovenia into the European Union and the elimination of customs borders, where tax controls on the movement of goods from and to Slovenia have also been carried out, there have been significant changes in the tax control system. The uncontrolled movement of goods and services within the European Union, and thus the large expansion of the common market, have also resulted in negative effects among the positive effects, in terms of increased tax evasion. Tax evasion, on the one hand, causes direct damage to the budget while, on the other hand, creating unfair competition for honest taxpayers and thus economic damage. In this context, by combining the Tax Administration of the Republic of Slovenia and the Customs Administration of the Republic of Slovenia into a new organisation, a more effective fight against such fraud can be expected. We probably all agree that repression alone cannot be combated against tax evasion, and a broad public awareness campaign on the consequences of tax evasion for the wider social community must also be conducted. Members of the New Slovenia Group will be supported by the proposal for a law on financial management.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u14	Respected and respected! Social Democrats are a coalition party. For us, parliamentary treatment of this law does not therefore begin today, but in fact, the law on financial administration has already undergone a significant grinding, assessment, confrontation of views, causes and consequences of the merger of the tax and customs administration into a new body empowered to collect taxes and other compulsory duties in the Republic of Slovenia. We are convinced that we have already contributed to the improvement of this law in the stage of our consideration so far, but as with others, there are also some challenges facing further coalition coordination. First of all, we want to talk to the Social Democrats about tax culture. To give Caesar what was Caesar's, we used to be able to do in these places. But it's not a tax to the emperor, it's not a tax to someone else. Social Democrats believe in the social state of Slovenia, we believe in its strong solidarity and justice principle. We want the responsible policy, the professional administration and good governance of all social subsystems to give all citizens and citizens an adequate equivalent for the taxes and taxes paid. We don't pay taxes for someone else. We do not pay contributions for someone else, but primarily to provide ourselves, our families and the whole community with the level of rights that we consider to be in line with the civil nature of this time. This is an extremely important emphasis. Increasingly, in Slovenia, the belief is confirmed that we have brought an important part of our social life and the national economy to a grey field of avoidance. It should also be pointed out that, in these cases, it is not primarily about cheating on the State, but rather about cheating on itself. We stress, however, that there is still much to be done in politics and throughout the public sector to ensure that the citizen of the economic operator or any tax resident of the Republic of Slovenia receives the corresponding equivalent of the tax paid, which he will be able to appreciate as a return service provided to him by the community. In adopting the Financial Administration Act, we want to warn the Social Democrats about the reorganisational property that we have been going to Slovenia for several years. Each government is trying to reorganize something, set up a new office, and a new coalition is coming, and it seems to it that this office or administration is unnecessary, and so on and on and on. The result of this process is flawed, ineffective institutions that become themselves intended, but no longer know how to carry out their broader mission for which they are established. An example of the former Social Accounting Service, which has already been mentioned for this speaker today, is a good example of the disassembly of institutions, the creation of something new, which does not ultimately bring optimal but often worse results than the great goals that were set at the beginning of this process. We therefore stress that today the Law on Financial Administration is before us as an act laying down the arrangements for a final situation, the final state of the day on which the Financial Administration of the Republic of Slovenia will be established. Only in rare provisions do these provisions relate to the process itself – a process that needs to be conducted responsibly, in order to create in the current customs administration and tax administration, on the other hand, between employees, between different business cultures and business processes within these institutions a suitable corporate culture, which will ultimately, with the high level of belonging and commitment of employees to objectives, with a good organisational structure that will deliver optimum results, be able to achieve the purpose of financial management. It is therefore all the more important in this area to train and strengthen the structure which will give the country an appropriate body for an effective tax collection system tomorrow. True, this law was drafted by the former government of Janš. In this too, we see the promise and hope that the next government will not start the process from scratch, this institution will rediscover and put the process back to zero. In this respect, it is important to develop a policy that relies on a certain degree of continuity and builds on improving existing proposals, not on their decomposition. It seems important to us to point out that intensive social bargaining has already taken place in the process of drafting this law. We find this issue particularly important and sensitive. It is true that we do not combine these two institutions because of the employees, but the employees in these two institutions are in the new financial administration of the Republic of Slovenia the tool, the power of the State to carry out tasks in this area effectively, responsible, fair and lawful. The laws are good and bad, but their implementation eventually stops at a man with public authority and responsibility to do his job well. In the case of the tax administration of the Republic of Slovenia, we can see that there are still two business cultures: one from the former Republic Public Payments Administration and the other from the former tax authorities. And the third, in fact, from the Social Accounting Service, which, to a large extent after its transformation, divided into IAPES on the one hand, public payments on the other and part into tax administration. Therefore, it is so important that the genesis of institutions comes at the level of the employees that these institutions create. Some discussions so far have also touched on the territorial, regional issues of organisation of future financial administrations. We agree that these aspects of compliance are valuable and that they need to be taken into account with precision not only in the process of pooling these institutions, but also in the wider range of future administrative arrangements of the country, that this regime must be as uniform as possible, but, on the other hand, through the development of services, in particular the development of e-services, follow the broadest accessibility of citizens and citizens to public administration services, including the tax service. We will therefore pay attention to this issue in further coordination. For the time being, however, it is also thanks to our law that a solution is in place that promises a more gradual, thoughtful and territorially balanced organisation of the financial administration. But on the other hand, while everyone in this room often demands rationalisation in public administration, let us not forget that we can be very quickly opposed to this process as soon as a part of this process touches our home place. Here it will be necessary to choose the appropriate sense and moderation in choosing between rationalisation, between the locationability of financial administration services and, on the other hand, the legitimate expectations of individual regions after the decentralised organisation of public administration. Social Democrats believe that the Financial Administration Act in many ways improves the set of tools for an effective system of collection of compulsory taxes and taxes. This will be because we have been particularly attentive to the ability of the state administration in the direction of modern financial police in recent years. At this stage, the Social Democrats will support the Financial Administration Act, and in the general debate and debate on the Committee, we will try to contribute as much as possible to the law so that, after the merger of these two institutions into the single financial administration of the Republic of Slovenia, Slovenia will have a good, highly motivated, effective tax and compulsory service, which will, in the first place, provide citizens with a sense of the appropriate equivalence of the fees paid in order to extend the voluntary nature of fulfilling obligations not only to the State but also to the community and to ourselves. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u36	Thank you so much for saying that. I must say that I am pleased with today's debate and that it has shown some principled agreement on most of the solutions that the proposal for a law brings. I would particularly like to respond to some specific questions raised in the debate. I will begin on a less-exposed issue of powers. In fact, the Financial Administration Act introduces the powers that the tax administration and, on the other hand, the customs administration have so far enjoyed at one stage. In particular, in the case of the customs administration, due to the specificity of the part of customs workers in the field, it is necessary to examine the goods covered by certain specific powers. These powers relate, on the one hand, to the collection of duties and also to other tasks to be performed by the customs service, for example in the area of control of illicit drug trafficking. In view of the fact that all these tasks will be transferred to the Financial Administration in the future, the Financial Administration Act also sets out in detail these powers for future servants of the Financial Administration. Of course, this does not mean that all employees of the financial administration will have the same powers and that, for example, weapons will go around Slovenia. No, no, no. The act on internal systemisation and job creation will specify where these powers and these additional resources can be used. People in these jobs will therefore be specially trained. It is not intended to extend the scope of these powers in any way in the light of the powers already conferred on individual staff. This does not mean a quiet establishment of the financial police, which was mentioned in the debate, but is essentially about preserving the possibility that employees of the future financial administration will maintain the possibility of doing what they have had so far. In the financial consequences of the bill, we have highlighted in particular the costs, direct costs that will arise in connection with mergers and are linked to the harmonisation of information systems between the current tax and customs services and the unification of certain solutions that the new service needs. Here all information solutions, as has been developed so far, remain in force, for example in the area of electronic monitoring of tax procedures, the so-called eDIS, as well as in the customs field or in excise duties. In particular, the excise and customs systems continue to be integrated into European information solutions, as well as the tax information system in the work relating to the exchange of tax data and the exchange of VAT liability data are linked to European information solutions. What needs to be consolidated needs to be consolidated in the essence of a purely organisational, informational solution between the two services. When will these expenses be reimbursed? It is difficult to quantify financially all the advantages that future financial management will have. The combined operation of both services in the last four months shows in essence the positive effects and additional revenue collected, within four months in the areas of four activities € 4.6 million of the additional value added tax. Of course, we are talking only about one segment of tax and customs activity, which has been the most visible to the public in the last four months. More, let's say, and the daily work on inspection controls remains hidden to the eyes as regards the identification of the so-called tax gap and the identification of the unjustified transfer of funds into tax havens. Finally, I would like to respond to the issue of regionalisation or regional organisation of future financial management. Many Members touched on the regulation on regional organisation of financial administration in their debate. Here let me draw attention to the fact that the solutions that we have included in the proposal for a law have to be based on the system regime of the state administration, where we have coordinated solutions with the Ministry of the Interior and have to follow the solution that the regional organisation of the service should be determined by a government regulation and not by law itself. Finally, it should be borne in mind that such arrangements are already in force today, under the Tax Service Act or the Customs Service Act. Why has the regulation not already been tabled together with other sub-legislative acts before the draft law? The 100th paragraph of the draft law, which essentially introduces a transitional period, should also be taken into account. The whole system of mergers is created by bringing the general tax and general customs office together within a period of four months after the adoption of the law in the National Assembly into the Single General Finance Office of the Republic of Slovenia, while regional organisation remains unchanged at the level of the tax or customs offices. The new management of the financial management is required to prepare further procedures and systematisation of jobs for the regional organisation of future financial management in the period from 1 January 2015. Until then, they shall remain both tax and customs offices, as organised by the current organisation, essentially in force. It is necessary to separate the proposal for a regulation last year in public treatment and the procedure has not been completed. These were two regulations that spoke on the one hand about the establishment of tax offices or customs offices. The majority of Members referred to this Regulation. These are regulations prepared under the applicable tax administration law or customs service law. We have not yet prepared a regulation on the basis of this law, because we have been preparing, at this point, those sub-legislative acts which must be adopted immediately or practically at the same time as the law itself has been enacted. As a result, solutions to the regional organisation of future financial administrations remain open. However, we will also present specific data or figures, indicating the uneven burden of individual organisational units, in the context of the second reading of this proposal, indicating the non-economic organisation in the current scope of the organisation and presenting what this point would mean for the proposed transformation, which, as it is said, is also a matter of further negotiation or discussion. Even today, we can say that the tax and customs offices are not entirely organised in accordance with administrative units or with the envisaged future administrative boundaries. It is a question of where the actual presence of the tax office or customs office is necessary, and where those needs that exist locally can be met by tax or customs exposure. On the one hand, we must take into account that the way in which the debtors operate is changing, that they are increasingly using modern information channels, but we also want to ensure physical presence at local level, and this can be ensured either through the tax and customs offices either through exhibitions or through offices. Last but not least, the analysis of the functioning of tax offices at this time shows that there are between 5 and 10 daily visits at each tax facility, which justifies the organisation in the form it is set up. Full horizontal links with future administrative boundaries, I do not think it is expected, because the need for a tax or customs office to be present is quite different from the need for other administrative tasks. And I think it is really necessary to look at the data here further and to assess rationally, on the basis of the data, what is appropriate in terms of efficiency or cost-effectiveness of the use of public funds. Why is there a fiscal investigation in the bill? The fiscal investigation is a kind of special mandate given to tax or customs officials and, in our opinion, it is a law governing the organisation and powers of financial administration, not a law governing the tax procedure. Thank you very much for the warning on editorial error in Article 108. We will try to agree that the second-reading amendment will remove this deficiency. As regards the comment on the amount of revenue collected in 2013, it is likely that this lower revenue collection cannot be attributed to the inefficiency of the tax or customs administration, but also to some other indicators which have proved less favourable than those taken into account when planning revenue with a budget rebalancing. In short, I find this first reading useful. I expect that we will continue in the same way in the second reading, and I predict that for the second reading we will provide more detailed information on the current regional organisation, including the implications of the thoughts we have at the Ministry regarding future organisation. However, it is certainly not our intention to hide anything, but simply in view of the way the debate is going on, our assessment was that things that will be dealt with subsequently, in an additional almost one-year transitional period, should be conceived or completed further, and that there is no point at this point in overheating situations or discussions with some preliminary proposals for solutions that we are thinking about, but are not yet fully coordinated or agreed upon. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u52	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Dear Members and Members! You have before you a proposal for a new law on the Slovenian State Holding, proposed by the Government for adoption. The reason why the Government is proposing a new law is mainly because the existing law has not come to life. At the same time, the Government is proposing some new solutions, which have proved to be poorly defined in the existing law. The key objectives of the new law before you today are as follows. Disengagement of the function of the State as the owner of capital investments from other functions of the State. The second objective pursued by the proposal for a law is to centralise the management of all the investments of the Republic of Slovenia in order to achieve stable ownership, reduce management costs, increase profitability and value of investments, while ensuring a clear division of powers and responsibilities regarding the management of state assets. The draft law also aims to ensure transparency in the management of the country's capital investments. Particular attention is paid to reducing the impact of stakeholders, political groups and corruption risks and conflicts of interest. To this end, a chapter in the law is specifically elaborated. We consider this to be one of the essential elements that strengthen the existing law. As mentioned above, the draft law provides for the centralisation of State investment management in a way that transforms the Slovenian Compensation Company into a Slovenian State holding company on the date of the entry into force of this Act and the Slovenian State holding company shall continue to manage the entire property of the State which is in its direct or direct ownership. Unlike the current law, with a view to facilitating the adoption of a strategy in the proposal for a new law, the types of investments already owned by the State are already mentioned, either directly or indirectly. The draft law defines so-called strategic investments, with these countries in addition to the economic achievement of certain strategic objectives. The proposal defines important investments as the second category. With these, the country wants to preserve key development factors in Slovenia. And the third set of investments is so-called portfolio investments with which the country achieves exclusively economic objectives. With the proposal for a law, we want to achieve a robust system of governance of state property in a way that is clear about who manages it and how it is managed. In the area of who manages it, particular emphasis should be placed on the way in which those responsible for the management of State property are appointed. The law strengthens the way it is called the Supervisory Board of the Slovenian State Holding, how the procedures for the selection of the administration of the Slovenian State Holding are carried out and, finally, it is specified how the Slovenian State Holding then appoints those responsible for the management of the companies under its intervention. In addition, to ensure and strengthen democratic control over the operation of SDH in the draft law, we introduce the so-called Economic and Social Expert Committee, which consists of seven representatives of trade unions. The meeting of the Supervisory Board of the Slovenian State Holding Board may be attended by both the Minister of Finance and the President of the Commission of the National Assembly for the supervision of public finances, without the possibility of voting, but in the monitoring function. The bill extends the jurisdiction of the Court of Auditors to companies where SDH has a directly or indirectly dominant share of voting rights. The SDH bill also reports to the National Assembly once a year. As mentioned above, we believe that the proposals of the law are being implemented by a stable system of governance of state property, which should not be exchanged with any government. The system shall be implemented in a manner that will ensure effective management of assets where the State retains ownership and professionally managed privatisation procedures where the strategy foresees resale. Thank you.

In these last few years, since 2010, since the reconciliation of pensions has been severely defiled or not at all, 50 million or a proportional balance has been applied after harmonisation to the current needs of the pension fund, which are otherwise to be covered by the State or the budget, in so far as the contribution manoeuvre is shown. This obligation of the State at annual level amounts to around 1.5 billion. Thus, at least for this part of the purchase, they would know for what purposes this money is spent. If we look at the needs of the pension fund and are aware of the current demographic situation and future demographic changes and, of course, the fact that the pressures on the budget to cover pension deficits are higher from year to year, of course, what we are proposing makes sense. Also due to the crisis and the consequent decrease in inflows to the pension fund, the fall in employment, the unresolved relationship between the pension fund and the budget, the lack of unpaid contributions and the lower rate of employers' contributions, the KAD and the 10% share of purchases from the sale of state assets are essential to compensate for the shortfall in pension funds. But it is, and we are well aware of this in the Desus Group that this alone cannot solve the problems of the pension fund. We must establish it by the end of this year. ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u57	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Parliamentary Group Positive Slovenia Mr Brane Golubović.
ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u58	Dear colleagues and colleagues, the Prime Minister, representatives of the Ministry, welcome! Today, when we discuss the Slovenian State Holding Act at first reading, it is worth noting, however, the long way of adopting this law. The Slovenian State Holding Act has already been passed by the previous coalition, but in practice the law has never come to life. At the same time, with the passed law proposed by the government of Janez Janša, we have also abolished the AUKN. So we now have status quo in the area of state property management. In a way, a vacuum where you don't know who, what, why. It should also be said that in positive Slovenia we have already proposed a law on the management of the country's capital investments as an opposition party in 2012 and have also put it into the legislative process following a regular procedure. The fact is, and this has been repeatedly said that in Positive Slovenia we were not satisfied with the work of the AUKN so far, because we considered that the then government, despite its sincere intentions and desire to prevent imprudent and lobbyist hunters from cohesively managing the state property that we have witnessed since Slovenia's entry into the European Union, underestimated the difficulty of transferring the management role from the government or the competent ministries to the AUKN. However, it must be acknowledged that this was the first serious attempt to pool all state property in one place. The work of the AUKN showed that it was also involved in the work of administrations past the control councils, and that the agency has become a remote centre, a power centre and thus an even easier prey for various interests, lobbying and politics. The previous proposal by the former Minister of Economy, who proposed the dismissal of members of the Agency's administration because of alleged corruption, is also confirmed. The former Prime Minister of the Government also notified the Agency to the Commission for the Prevention of Corruption, which was why our fundamental objective was to decide, on the basis of a broad public debate, to pool all state assets under one roof and to ensure transparent governance and control of governance, thereby preventing different stakeholders from having an impact on state property. At that time, the ruling coalition of our marriage did not support him and rejected him on his mother’s work force and went to accept his marriage. However, the government, the then government, because of the political crisis that was triggered by the then President of the Government, Janez Janša, with the announcement of a vote of confidence, has prevented the adoption, in our opinion, of a law that has not been drafted. Thus, we all gained additional time and a new opportunity together. That is also what the then government learned. At the invitation of the Government of Janez Janša as a constructive opposition, the positive Slovenia participated in the working group of representatives of parliamentary parties and the Government in the summer of 2012 coordinating the text of the Slovenian State Holding Act. The text of the law received many substantive and technical corrections, which satisfactorily solved some of the dilemmas but still large differences remained. Since it was obvious that agreements would probably not have been reached, even if they had continued to be coordinated, a decision had been taken to continue the consideration in the National Assembly or to decide on these open issues. In the debate and in public, Positive Slovenia at that time advocated the need to allow greater participation of the opposition and the social partners in the supervisory authorities, to allow the Court of Auditors to review the information of a public nature, to take into account the Public Procurement Act, to change the decision-making of the Supervisory Board regarding the recapitalisation of existing and potential new purchases of assets and so on. In addition to all of this, the question of the possible unconstitutional nature of the holding was raised because the income of the holding company from the sale of the State's capital investments would not be the revenue of the State budget. For the reason stated in Positive Slovenia in September 2012, we decided not to support the proposal and to apply for the invitation to tender for the subsequent legislative referendum. There was no call for a referendum on the basis of a decision of the Constitutional Court to have an unconstitutional effect on the referendum. We respected the decision to disagree with her. So, as you can see, our problem with the Slovenian State Holding Act was not a proposed status form for the management of state property in one place, but we differed mainly in how to ensure the greatest possible transparency of governance and control. The law, which came into force at the end of 2012, as I have already said, has never lived in practice. Therefore, when the Government was formed, the Positive Slovenia continued its efforts to enact a law that would allow transparent management of state assets in one place, with the aim of pursuing strategic and economic objectives and maintaining key development factors of the country and effective control of the work of SDH. Let me stress that the purpose of the law is not to sell state property, as some say. The purpose of the Slovenian State Holding is to collect all state property in one place, transparently manage it and ensure control of this management. With regard to coalition coordination, I would like to say that they were — they were normal and they were on the street. One of the parties coordinated through the media and achieved what she wanted. It has achieved that KAD remains a single legal person who will later be transformed into a demographic fund. This is not in line with the Government's wishes, but it was in line with one of the coalition partners, DeSUS. The draft law also includes an article on the annual reporting of SDH to the National Assembly. The most important news is also the addition of an article which provides that employees and representatives of the Council of Workers should be informed in due time in the event of the sale of a majority share. The law will also be subject to the newly adopted Law on Access to Public Information, so that the public will have access to certain data. SDH will be able to supervise the Commission to prevent corruption. One of the most important, however, is the composition of the Supervisory Board, which will be composed of five members on a proposal from the expert commission and will be appointed by the State Council on a proposal from the Government. The expert committee, which will propose members of the Supervisory Board on the basis of strict criteria, will consist of experts with experience from cooperative management, management of human resources resources and the functioning of supervisory boards. In addition, the expert committee shall carry out the procedure for obtaining candidates for members of the Supervisory Board and shall evaluate the candidates and shall finally draw up a proposal for members of the Supervisory Board submitted by the Ministry of Finance. The members of the Supervisory Board must meet conditions such as appropriate work experience, adequate education, professional knowledge and competences, non-conviction, non-compatibilities with a function in a political party, personal integrity, and high business ethics must be a business success and have a reputation in society. Even stricter criteria are set for members of the board. The law also sets out what is important and contributes to the transparency of the appointment of members of the Supervisory Board, that if the Ministry or Government appoints a member of the Supervisory Board or proposes to a member of the Supervisory Board of the National Assembly, which has not been delegated by the expert committee, it must be made public and, of course, it is logical to also justify why it did so. With regard to the Supervisory Council, the solution must also be given to allow the President of the Commission of the National Assembly for the supervision of public finances, which comes from the opposition, to attend its meetings in addition to the Minister. Therefore, the opposition will also have the opportunity to participate in the meetings of the Supervisory Committee, otherwise without the right to vote. You are likely to see how much attention is being paid to the composition of the Supervisory Council, a world which, in my opinion, must play a key role, as it appoints the Administration and then supervises the achievement of our common goals, the objectives of the Republic of Slovenia and thus of all generations. The law, however, introduces a new advisory body on a proposal from trade unions, namely an economic and socio-professional committee composed of representatives of trade unions and has the status of an advisory body of the SDH administration. In addition, the law provides for the supervision of the Court of Auditors. Following the adoption of this law, the Court of Auditors will be able to revise not only HSE but also TES. With these provisions, we have ensured that an effective mechanism for controlling the functioning of SDH is in place through state independent institutions, professions, workers, trade unions and also through the National Assembly and the Government. Basically, and this is extremely important to decide how to manage assets and what can be sold and under what conditions, the adoption of the investment management strategy, proposed by the Government, will be adopted by the National Assembly. Investments are divided into three categories: strategic investments, significant investments, portfolio investments. Dear colleagues and colleagues, the idea of the law is extremely good because it contains very clear provisions on how assets are managed transparently, how SDH works are controlled, how and to whom SDH authorities must report, what conditions the members of the administration and supervisors must meet, how the public participates, how the investment management strategy is adopted and what it should contain. Unfortunately, not everything is in marriage, and unfortunately not everything is in the rules. In the end, the law is enforced by people and if they do not have a sense of responsibility for the common property, to the state, if they do not have high personal integrity and business ethics in addition to, of course, excellent knowledge and experience for the management of businesses, then no, even so well-designed and prepared law will help. It all depends on people, and people are the ones who create the community we live in. And people are also those who create the image and business successes of the company, all employees without exception. The law is highly coordinated. Nevertheless, certain questions remain open and I believe that today we will open up these issues to all, discuss them, hear and listen and try to find optimal solutions. What are the questions that I personally expect us to discuss today? One of the questions is how to establish an active relationship between the so-called bad bank and SDH, which could take over the healthy cores of companies that are at a bad bank. Secondly, the question is whether the transfer of all-owned SDH is good or bad. Now we have a mixed combination. In addition, the largest amount of dust currently raises the area of determining the level of salaries of managers and the way they are rewarded. Personally, I do not see any problems if we tie the highest wage in the company to the lowest or possibly the average wage in the company. Personally, I am committed to establishing the relationship between the highest and lowest wages in the company, which will be appropriate and stimulating in time for both leadership and other employees. I will also listen here to all the arguments of colleagues and colleagues. I would also like to include in this debate the award and associated criteria that will be truly tangible and will be linked, for example, to the increased value of the company or something like that. Motivation is not only important for management personnel, but it is the same if not even more important for all employees in these companies. People have to feel like they belong to the company, they have to see a perspective in the company, because it's the only way they can be active and creative, and they're willing to put up with something for the company's success. If anyone, then the state and SDH must make it possible. All employees are responsible for the success of the company. Of course, I would like to see personally that all employees are rewarded at the same time when they are rewarded with good results. But I am saddened by what we are witnessing in the media, and this is not the direction of Slovenia, when some owners and at the same time managers – the example of the manager of the technology company and the owner – are doing business with profits, but in a kind of negative way stimulate their employees. Such mode of action should be clearly condemned and such mode of action does not fall within SDH. The modern Robin Hoods, who take the poor and give to the rich, are something that Slovenia does not deserve. This is something that is not in accordance with Article 67 of the Constitution, which states that private property in Slovenia also has a social and environmental function. So, colleagues and colleagues, if I conclude, I look forward to the debate that we will be witnessing to her today. Some questions are still open, but I believe that we will hear good arguments and that in the end, in a few weeks when we adopt this law, we will decide for the benefit of all the inhabitants and residents of the Republic of Slovenia who own this state property. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u62	Respected and respected! And a good extension for consensus, to agree, is that the new political team before this national assembly does not come with a completely new concept, but rather with the upgrade of the previous Slovenian State Holding Act, because it is trying to find a reasonable compromise between the current regime in force at the time of the Janšev government, the concept before that, following the Capital Investment Management Act and the Agency, with the desire to achieve a reasonable measure of centralisation of the management of state assets on the one hand, professionality and efficiency and transparency of governance, in order to achieve the greatest and optimal benefits for the country, economy and its people. For Social Democrats, the issue of governance of state property is a first-class political theme. Not because we would have any special interests in the management of this or that company, but because we are aware that we have a special responsibility as the current generation to this property. Long to generations who, with their work and knowledge, have created this property, as well as the debt to future generations, which of this property must offer from national wealth at least as good a chance for development as we have received ourselves. This, of course, cannot be done without the consistent, strict and decisive enforcement of good corporate governance of the entire state property. The less we do in this area, the greater the need for privatisation. The more we give up and are subjected to interesting or political pressure to manage state investments, the more the argument is that we sell as much of this property as possible. We are opposed to social democrats selling state property. However, we are open to reasonable privatisation, which, from case to case, will assess from business to business what the impact of an individual company on the national economy, its development, the position of employees, the role of the most demanding jobs, especially in development, and the wider social responsibility that every smart undertaking must have in our opinion. That is why we do not question the decisions already made. We understand, however, that the decision on which to sell an undertaking only to consider it as a portfolio investment and to choose the optimum moment between the date of purchase of a particular asset and the date of sale in order to achieve the maximum effect on the budget, between the question which enterprise is important for the country because it has a broader development impact for a country or for a particular region, has a high concentration of domestic knowledge in its products and employs a highly qualified professional profile, or the question that an entity is as a strategic one which the State, as an active owner and manager, wants to keep in its possession in the long term. This classification of individual tasks cannot be decided on the basis of a simple table indicating investments, defining the importance of portfolios or the strategic nature of investments and thus the decision, we sell it, we keep it, we will wait here a little longer. It's a strategic decision. And this strategic decision must be made on the basis of an expert opinion, a sober and broad public opinion and a unanimous decision by political stakeholders. I therefore insist that the Slovenian State Holding Act definitively enforce the principle that there must be a strategic issue ahead of the form. We estimate that in the last period we are a few years ahead of the third attempt to re-orient the governance of state assets. But in all these three attempts, we haven't come up with what we're missing in the whole process. This is the definition of a strategy, not just a document, but the logical and systematic wording of what the country expects from its investments as the owner. Since the state had not been able to form its will through collective formation until now, state property had been left to the positions of the supervisory councils and administrations, and it has often become a hostage of political interest, and in many cases their opposition has arisen from good companies. It is unacceptable for us to decide within three months of the adoption of the type of individual investment law without a comprehensive strategy, with all the elements to be included. We ask the Government to do its utmost to present a strategy by the end of March, and no later than three months after the adoption of the law before this National Assembly, a comprehensive document containing all the elements necessary for the National Assembly to decide on the nature and fate of the individual investments. We demand that all key classification decisions, i.e. the classification of individual investments, be taken by the National Assembly. This is a task that comes to the highest area of democracy, because it is actually an inherent issue of governance. Also because we believe in the so-called democratic model of governance of state property, which is now increasingly prevalent in the modern world. In the meantime, until the adoption of the strategy, we reject any solution that would reduce the standard of democratic decision-making on the fate of individual investments. We allow economic and other needs to give rise to the need for interim consideration, but even then such decisions must be taken in the National Assembly. This assures their public, this ensures a broad debate and as Democrats we also believe in making optimal decisions for society. We expect that the Slovenian State Holding Act will implement the coalition principle enshrined in the first coalition agreement between coalition partners, and that is to say at least part of the purchases from the sale of that property that the State will decide to sell on the basis of a careful assessment of the strategic nature of the investment to the new development. Today, we have a number of needs in the economy, in the financing of operating assets, in new investments or in the launching of entirely new entrepreneurial projects for our young talented people who are not listening to capital markets. We want the country to strengthen its role in launching new projects, in launching projects from which we can hope for new jobs in the future. The law should not only provide for organisation; the law must also define the quality of governance significantly more than it has so far. If not by law, this issue will have to be addressed through a strategy. Today, there are different methodologies in the world, ways to measure the effects of public property management and at least the strategy should summarize the best models by which the country, as the owner, can effectively and successfully follow the objectives set by the strategy: governance standards, performance criteria, capital returns, number of employees, productivity and so on. In the views of the previous parliamentary groups of our coalition partners, we have heard a lot of words about the role of workers, that people are creating a business outcome. We would like this understanding to find the place of representatives of employees across the entire vertical, from employees in holding companies and individual subsidiaries, i.e. in individual investments, including in the supervisory board of the Slovenian State Holding. In this place, we set an example for such co-management from Slovenia. Workers and employees in all companies owned by Holdinga Slovenian power plants today, on the principle of one worker – one vote elects their representative in the Supervisory Board of Holding. This form of co-ordination is not only an initial trade union fight and the protection of labour rights, it is seen as an important correction to the possible political impact on the management of state property. Even though we believe that the law represents good restrictions on such interventions, we are realistic and believe that such practices should also be prevented by properly defining the participation of workers in management. However, the law provides for the establishment of an economic and social expert committee, which we consider to be the supervision of the legality and realization of the wider interest represented by state property in accordance with the democratic governance model already mentioned. Such an understanding of this expert committee was presented by the Secretary of State today in the introductory explanation. The social partners in the holding therefore do not exercise the mission and cooperation of workers in managing, but the manifestation of those interests of stakeholders who need to look after the legality, prudence, economy and efficiency of the management of state assets, so that we can end up saying that we have achieved a good model of governance of state property, which also returns maximum benefits to the State in the form of a return to the state budget. The issue of nomination and accreditation, the question of how to reach the best candidates for members of the supervisory boards or members of management boards in holding, as well as in companies in ownership or management, is an extremely important issue. ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u78	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. A few questions were raised in the course of the debate and I would like to present the Government's opinion to the point of view. On the final question, what is the status of this law and how the Government monitors the consideration of this law in the National Assembly. It is quite clear that the bill before you is harmonised in the Government and as such has been forwarded to the National Assembly. If I were not in agreement, this proposal would not be before you today. Of course, the Government is fully committed to democratic procedures in Parliament and in the course of the parliamentary debate, I have no doubt that any solutions in this law can be improved. We are very open to all good proposals to improve and strengthen this text, but each time a majority will determine what will and will not be adopted for each individual amendment. Why can the Government believe that the management of assets, as proposed by this law, can be effective and that it will be effective? For the first time because it is very detailed and in our opinion it is professionally determined who manages this property, how these persons are chosen to manage this property, and how they manage it. If I'm going to do this first, how do I manage this property? The law clearly lays down the competence of SDH for comprehensive management, which means the acquisition and disposal of this property on behalf of and on behalf of the Republic of Slovenia when it comes to the property of the Republic of Slovenia. In the case of property transferred from the Soda to SDH on its own behalf, as well as in the case of property relating to KAD. So SDH has all the power to manage all the assets entrusted to it in this law. Why we believe that the management of this property can be effective, but it may be more important who manages it. This law is much more detailed on how we get to the competent owners of the state's assets. There is no procedure to allow the Government to determine, say, candidates for the SDH Supervisory Board. It is clearly defined how this appointment takes place. The expert committee proposing qualified candidates must operate at the back, and the criteria for these candidates must be set. The criteria set out are that these candidates must not only be individually competent but also collectively, i.e. that their knowledge is complementary. Therefore, the Supervisory Board of five members will be able to act and cover complementaryly the different areas relevant to the functioning of the State holding company. Furthermore, a number of suggestions regarding international calls were heard in the debate. The law provides that an international invitation to tender or an international human resources agency shall be issued for the appointment of SDH administration. This means that we can reach significantly more competent people, those who will be able to manage our assets in a responsible manner. The issue of independence of SDH was raised, given that the law provides for the possibility of the participation of the President of the Commission for the supervision of public finances and the Minister of Finance at the meetings of the Supervisory Board, of course without the possibility of making a decision, only in the role of observers. Here we have to draw a parallel with the Bank of Slovenia Act. It also provides that the Minister of Finance may participate in the meetings of the Bank of Slovenia Council. It provides that the President of the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy of the National Assembly may participate in the meetings of the Council of the Bank of Slovenia and that no one is likely to think that the Bank of Slovenia is in any way limited in its independence by the participation of the two representatives. Even more, we have the same practice from the European Central Bank. The President of the Euro Group may participate in meetings of the Governing Council of the ECB. And the Commissioner responsible for monetary and financial matters in the EU can participate, and therefore no one doubts the independence of the European Central Bank. Therefore, we believe that the participation of the Minister and the President of the Commission for the supervision of public finances at SDH supervisory board meetings will in no way violate the provision of SDH independence in the management of state property. Several questions were raised regarding privatisation. The government is in the explanations of the law, and I myself wanted to point out in the introductory explanations why we believe that SDH, as a professional asset manager, is the appropriate platform for privatisation. What we're going to privatize, what we're going to sell, is going to be a matter of debate. And this debate will take place here in the National Assembly and the National Assembly will be fully involved in this regard, as it was included in the listing of 15 companies currently in the privatisation process. And for these 15 companies, criteria have also been set for what needs to be pursued through the privatisation process. Clearly, the first criterion is to maximize the purchase. At the same time, the decree clearly lays down other criteria which the DRC, as the main contractor of the privatisation process, must now provide in the privatisation process. These other criteria relate to broader macroeconomic objectives, including employment provision, including the provision of development potentials in Slovenia, including further investment and the development of privatised companies. The next set of questions addressed relates to the patching of the pension allowance. I think that at this point we must point out that, however, it does not apply to working as much as KAD can in the past or in the future contribute to the sustainability of the pension fund. If you look at the incomes of the Pension and Disability Insurance Office today, you will see that about 3.2, 3.3 billion are collected with contributions, about 1.6 billion is added to the state budget, about 50 million is added to the KAD. If you want this in percentages, about 70% is due to contributions, little less than 30% is the transfer of the state budget and about 1% is the transfer of Kad to ZPIZ. Expecting that KAD in any form can contribute significantly to the sustainability of public finances in the long run is a illusion. That's what I think we have to admit. We maintain the basic concept of Kad or the basic purpose of why KAD was established through this law, we also maintain the requirement of transferring 50 million to PPIZ at the annual level so that we can imagine that this will significantly change the sustainability of our pension or public financial system in the long run, but it is only right that we all recognize that this is not the case. Another issue was raised, namely the issue of financing. So SDH will, already the current law of SDH foresees that in the interim period of the SDD part of the management is performed on behalf of and on behalf of the Republic of Slovenia, so it is a matter of payment, assuming that this is also a new proposal, which is before you today, foresees that SDH is covering the management costs of this asset and does not keep the statement that this money is not provided anywhere in the budget today. In particular, item 9521 The cost of managing the country's financial assets in this year's budget provides for a level of funding of approximately EUR 1.2 million, which is intended to cover the costs of managing the country's financial assets. So much for this stage. Thank you very much.

Bank secrecy means a commitment by the bank not to disclose to third parties information on the property status of the commissioners, except in exceptional cases provided for by law. The basic purpose of bank secrecy must be based on the above-mentioned basis. As with each institution, bank secrecy must also have adequate safeguards that prevent the misuse of bank secrecy to cover up tax evasion, money laundering, non-execution and even cover up illegal or criminal acts. The legal arrangements for exemptions from bank secrecy must therefore be laid down in such a way as to allow supervisory and other State authorities access to data that enable the effective management of their powers. Banka Slovenije has the most important supervisory function over banks and other credit institutions. Of course, when we talk about the banking system, we must be aware that we are part of the Eurosystem; it is said that the legal solutions must be compatible with the Eurosystem's definition. The National Assembly has an important supervisory function in the banking system, Banka Slovenije is obliged to report to the National Assembly once a year in its operations, and the function of the National Assembly is even more important than the legislative body that decides on the use of public funds. The function of the National Assembly is also to determine the responsibility of supervisors to create such a situation. ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u120	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Dear Mr. Minister with colleagues, distinguished colleagues, colleagues! Today we are considering a proposal to supplement Article 215 of the Banking Act, although the existing regime is appropriate and allows detection and prosecution authorities to disclose the information they need to determine liability in the event of irregularities in banking. When we considered the decision to liquidate the Probank and the Bank Factor, the applicant requested data which, in all countries, democratic and even those slightly less democratic, are described as bank secrecy. So for at least six months, we have been hearing about the reasons for revealing the eligibility to disclose, I stress, bank secrecy. And what is bank secrecy? Bank secrecy is the bank's business secret; it is the basic assumption of every stable banking system. However, the concept of bank secrecy implies the bank's legal duty to protect the data on the property status of its customers and to refuse any third party request for the transmission of such information. Exceptions to the obligation to protect data may only be determined by law or under the authority of the Commissioner. Of course, we are aware that the commentants would not deposit money into the bank and thus trust it with information about their assets if the bank could not secure the trust of bank transactions. Under the terms of banking secrecy, we understand all the data and facts about the Commissioner, the facts and circumstances with which the Bank is located, irrespective of how it obtained these data. Members of the authorities, shareholders or shareholders and employees of the bank or other persons who, in relation to their work in the bank or in the management of the services of the bank, have in any way access to confidential information from the parties, shall not communicate such data to third parties, nor shall they themselves use or enable them to be used by third parties, except in cases specified by law. These prohibitions are defined in Article 215 of the Banking Act. Today, we will consider a proposal to supplement this article, which will also open confidential bank data to the National Assembly, its investigative bodies and other working bodies. Just so, unselectively, but perhaps we would limit ourselves only to banks that have received state aid, even some selection. Of course, according to our own conscience, we would decide which data to demand, use, and also manipulate them. Your Honor, this is a populism but anticipated move that does not surprise us. Some, on the other hand, sincerely believe that they have absolute authority in their hands to accuse and condemn, without judgment. At the same time, one wonders, of course, who needs the Constitution and the distribution of power, who needs the police, the prosecution and the judiciary, or the PKK or anyone. Of course, you are willing to change even the laws, only to achieve the goal and you have legitimate work. For us the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service and the practice of the whole world is important, which is of course normal. Just as it is important to comply with constitutional provisions, it is important to trust that banks that have received state aid do not fail. ParlaMint-SI_2014-01-31-SDZ6-Redna-21.ana.u148	Thank you so much for saying that. Just a brief explanation. We believe that the relevant authorities will be able to obtain all the necessary information on their own in order to detect irregularities in the Slovenian banking system in this way. In addition, the last amendment to the Banking Act also gave rise to an extension of the limitation of the age of criminal and compensation liability, a fourfold increase in the possibility of people avoiding their responsibilities through this institute. With regard to the disclosures, as mentioned in the debate today, I would also like to point out, on behalf of the Government, the amendments tabled on the proposal of the Law on Access to Public Information, which is currently in Parliament's hearing, allowing claims transferred from banks to a bad bank to the Bank's Claims Management Company to be subject to a significantly higher degree of transparency; not only will exposures to the Commissioners be published, gross exposures to the Commissioners who have been transferred to the Bank's Claims Management Company, but individual data from individual transactions contracts be disclosed, so will the way in which the transaction is concluded, who has approved the individual transactions, what insurances were used for the establishment of a particular transaction. In this way, we believe that maximum transparency in relation to the operation of a bad bank in relation to transfers made to a bad bank will be ensured in a legally regulated manner, and thus a better public view of its functioning. Thank you.
